Additional environment probe: `PUBLIC_BILLING_ENABLED=false npm run test -- --maxWorkers=2 src/lib/components/SubscriptionBanner.test.ts src/routes/auth/sign-up/page.server.test.ts` exits 1 in 0.635 s: sign-up's 15 tests pass; all three SubscriptionBanner tests fail because the banner is disabled. This confirms test expectations depend on the billing=true local overlay and fail under CI's declared billing=false setting. These banner tests retire with billing; this audit changed neither environment nor expectations.

`npm run format` intentionally not run: it writes existing files and Prompt 00 permits planning edits only. `format:check` is the safe baseline, not an implementation waiver. DB tests, E2E, Supabase start/reset/migrate, installs, preview login and remote services were not run. The default worker exit was not diagnosed as an application crash; a constrained rerun established complete test results without weakening configuration.

Three reproduced failures in `src/routes/org/[orgId]/settings/members/membersSettings.server.test.ts`, suite `members settings setup-state lookup degradation`:

- `still renders the members screen when the setup-state lookup fails`: expected one invitation, got zero.
- `keeps reporting the invitation-email stage when the setup-state lookup fails`: undefined row's `funnelStatusLabel`.
- `reads platform setup state when the lookup is available`: same missing-row property failure.

The file defines `NOW = 2026-08-04T12:00:00Z` and an invitation expiring `2026-08-15T09:00:00Z`. Pure helper cases receive a clock; these loader cases do not freeze loader time. No assertions or clocks were changed. Local environment notes independently describe these prior failures; this audit reproduced them at the current date.

Build warnings: a chunk exceeds 500 kB; Vercel dependency tracing cannot locate optional `@opentelemetry/api` from `@supabase/supabase-js/dist/index.mjs`. The latter is not evidence of runtime failure. No warning limit/dependency was changed.

Raw local logs and audit scratch code are at `/tmp/t3-stabilization-282fcace/` (`results.jsonl`, `format-check.log`, `lint.log`, `governance-check.log`, `check.log`, `test.log`, `test-workers2.log`, `build.log`, `inventory.cjs`, `inventory.json`). They are ephemeral and are not required to understand these results. Do not commit raw logs, local env, generated output or fixture credentials.

## Inventory method and limits

Started with `git status --short`, `git branch --show-current`, `git rev-parse HEAD`, `git worktree list`, sanitized origin inspection and `git ls-files`. Repeated checkout commands against the local V2 reference. Tracked-file enumeration is the source inventory; `rg`/`git grep` locate symbols, followed by implementation reads. Ignored config was enumerated with `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard` and inspected only for variable names, set/empty status, billing flag and loopback classification. No secret value or personnel row was printed. Lifecycle, CI, Vite/Svelte/Vitest, Supabase, Vercel and environment action files were read before safe checks.

Import inspection used installed TypeScript `createSourceFile` / `forEachChild` over all tracked TS/JS and Svelte `<script>` contents; collected import declarations, re-exports and string-literal dynamic imports; resolved `$lib`, `$features`, relative paths, `.js` to `.ts` and directory indexes. Strongly connected components were computed from resolved imports. Counts include tests and type imports. Nonliteral imports, template semantics, package exports, generated route types and actual bundle runtime reachability are not established by this graph. Cycles were individually inspected to distinguish type-only reverse edges from value cycles. This was scratch analysis, not a new repository governance tool.

Useful repeatable discovery commands (exclude generated/ignored source by starting from tracked files):

```sh
git ls-files src/routes src/features src/lib supabase/migrations supabase/tests e2e
git grep -n -E 'PUBLIC_BILLING_ENABLED|isBillingEnabled|SubscriptionPort|readOnlyGuard|PlanPolicy|gift_tier' -- src
git grep -n -E 'duty_roster|duty_event|advanced_duty|sourceLineage|da6_' -- src supabase/migrations
git grep -n -E 'E2E_TESTING|playwright|test:e2e' -- ':!package-lock.json'
git grep -n -E 'CREATE.*FUNCTION|CREATE.*TRIGGER|CREATE POLICY|FOREIGN KEY' -- supabase/migrations
```

Searches are candidate discovery, not deletion rules. Billing-pattern scan matched 144 non-`.test` TS/Svelte files, duty-pattern scan 102; those counts include comments/test support and omit unmatching reverse consumers. Exact semantic seams are in BASELINE. Do not turn these counts into thresholds. No claim is made that every test assertion or SQL overload was exhaustively reviewed.

### Schema ownership inventory

Text enumeration across 127 migrations found 70 distinct declared public table names, 182 function-definition occurrences (105 distinct names), 26 trigger-definition occurrences, 267 policy-definition occurrences (237 distinct names). Names can have overloads/redefinitions/drops, so these are **history counts, not active catalog counts**. Known retired tables in that history are `counseling_types`, `development_goals`, `registration_invites`; old Leaders Book/demo/clinic RPCs also require last-definition/drop interpretation. No catalog was queried.

Retiring their removed-feature callers does not make shared functions deletion targets. `supabase/tests` currently covers auth workflows, Calendar date indexes, core/ICT tenant isolation, ownership RPCs, billing snapshots, invitation acceptance, rank catalog, Personnel artifacts, audit timestamp index, sensitive RPC privileges and sign-in-roster scope.

## Performance evidence

Production build used billing false and synthetic values shown above. All client JS files: **290**, **4,727,769 bytes raw**, **1,421,898 bytes sum of individually gzip-compressed files**. Largest is ExcelJS `node_modules/exceljs/dist/exceljs.min.js`, emitted as `_app/immutable/chunks/DOle8-7O.js`: **929,913 raw / 254,430 gzip bytes**. These are build artifacts, not bytes transferred by any user journey.

Manifest diagnostic: following static `imports` from the page and known layout nodes (including root node 0), deduplicating emitted JS filenames, gives:

| Route                  | Node IDs     | JS files | Raw bytes | Sum gzip bytes |
| ---------------------- | ------------ | -------- | --------- | -------------- |
| Calendar               | 0, 4, 7, 49  | 97       | 676,643   | 225,022        |
| Personnel              | 0, 4, 11, 66 | 92       | 584,237   | 198,239        |
| Organization Dashboard | 0, 4, 6, 44  | 81       | 531,980   | 181,125        |
| Training reports       | 0, 4, 14, 86 | 101      | 713,803   | 234,542        |
| Critical Tasks reports | 0, 4, 8, 57  | 100      | 1,136,463 | 371,379        |

Method: resolve routes/nodes in `.svelte-kit/generated/client/app.js`, then traverse `.svelte-kit/output/client/.vite/manifest.json` entries `.svelte-kit/generated/client-optimized/nodes/<id>.js`. Sum file sizes and Python `gzip.compress` output per file. Excludes dynamic imports, app entry/runtime not reachable through those nodes, CSS/fonts, data/HTML, HTTP encoding/headers, caching and preload/navigation behavior. Node IDs/hashes apply only to this build. Thus these totals are static dependency diagnostics, **not complete cold-load or actual transferred-JS measurements**, and do not establish speed.

| Requested measurement                 | Calendar                                            | Personnel                                         | Dashboard                                  | Large lists / reports                            |
| ------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------ |
| Network versus server time            | Not measured; server timing helper exists           | Not measured                                      | Not measured; summary/support timer exists | Not measured; Training timer exists              |
| HTML/data payload                     | Not measured                                        | Not measured                                      | Not measured                               | Not measured                                     |
| Actual transferred JS                 | Not measured; manifest diagnostic above             | Same                                              | Same                                       | Same                                             |
| Hydration / main-thread work          | Not measured                                        | Not measured                                      | Not measured                               | Not measured                                     |
| Repeat requests / interaction latency | Not measured; serial/deduplication guards inspected | Duplicate bootstrap read observed, timing unknown | Not measured                               | Unbounded source-load candidates, timing unknown |

No callable browser/computer-use tool was available. `.mcp.json` declares a Svelte MCP server, but `list-sections`, `get-documentation`, and `svelte-autofixer` were not exposed in this session's tool catalog; no Svelte code was written. No preview using real or existing local data was started. The environment guide's small synthetic fixture was not accessed or enlarged; authenticated sessions can themselves mutate local auth state. These limitations are work inputs for later authorized verification, not evidence of good or bad runtime performance.

## Task 02-01: Deterministic invitation expiry

September 16, 2026 Anchorage / September 17 UTC; diff from `87d9866cc84422f597bc2481157163c7e95f1cac`. Node `v24.18.0`, npm `11.17.0` (repository declares `11.12.1`), existing installed dependencies; no installation or lockfile change in this task. Root instructions apply; no nested AGENTS found. Read retained R1–R8, the baseline test-disposition inventory, F04 and prior task evidence, the verification contract, and the relevant invitation/status contract in ADR 0012. Program decisions supersede the ADR's legacy F01 preservation wording.

**Phase activation and refinement:** Phase 01's documentation acceptance passed in its recorded evidence; its retained-test failure was explicitly assigned to 02-01. Robert authorized activation after that acceptance. Phase 02 now has bounded safety-net, SQL/RLS, assertion-cutover and complete E2E-removal tasks. The dependency order requires useful protection before deleting low-value assertions. High-risk request/scope, acquisition, Organization creation, Calendar CRUD and retained workflow obligations have named owners; F01/F02/F06 remain repair gaps rather than desired test outcomes. All E2E code/config/mail/seed/runtime consumers, npm lockfile regeneration, clean install and verification without E2E values are owned by 02-05. Phase 03 remains inactive.

**Changed:** Scoped fake time to the three members-settings loader degradation tests with `beforeEach` using the existing `NOW = 2026-08-04T12:00:00Z`; `afterEach` restores real timers and owned console spies. The real loader and shaping implementation run against fake store/setup-state ports. The two injected lookup failures must still leave an actionable invitation and preserve the failed invitation-email reason; successful lookup must still report the setup-email failure. This protects R3/R5 optional status degradation without changing production expiry semantics.

**Deleted:** Implicit host-clock dependency and leaked console-spy state in this suite. No assertions, fixture dates, implementation/configuration files or E2E artifacts deleted in 02-01; no runtime or schema changes.

**Detection evidence:** `npm run test -- 'src/routes/org/[orgId]/settings/members/membersSettings.server.test.ts'` initially exited 1: 25 passed, three failed. The August-expiring fixture disappeared at September host time, producing one missing-invitation assertion and two undefined-status failures. The same command after the scoped clock fix exited 0: all 28 passed (0.163 s Vitest). No fixture extension, changed expected value, skipped assertion, function-under-test mock or temporary production fault was used.

**Acceptance: passed.** All existing file assertions now execute at their declared fixture time and the full retained suite passes. Clock/spies are restored after each loader case; production behavior, tenant scope and test inventory are unchanged. The before/after evidence detects the clock regression without creating a new test solely to assert test setup.

## Task 02-01a: Rank dialog teardown error

September 16, 2026; diff from `16047c179182f9213080114b5f0d6771522b1932`. Node `v24.18.0`, npm `11.17.0` (declared `11.12.1`), Vitest `4.1.8`, happy-dom `20.9.0`, Svelte `5.55.8`, bits-ui `2.18.1`; existing installed dependencies. Root instructions apply; no nested AGENTS found. Read the active queue, retained contracts (especially R7), prior teardown evidence, program decisions and verification contract.

**Cause and cutover:** The rank test suite synchronously unmounted its components, leaving bits-ui's deferred body-style restoration pending when Vitest could release the document. The suite now owns fake timers from setup through asynchronous cleanup: unmount, execute pending callbacks, assert no timers remain, then restore real timers in `finally`. This replaces the synchronous teardown for all rank cases. No timer cancellation, error suppression, skipped assertion or dependency patch was added. The original three rank behavior tests and production actions/dialogs remain intact; no runtime, schema, configuration or dependency consumer needs a cutover.

**Detection:** `npm run test -- 'src/routes/org/[orgId]/settings/ranks/page.svelte.test.ts'` passed the three original tests in one uncontrolled baseline run; the historical full-suite scheduling race was not reproduced naturally in that run. Two controlled regression cases open the real Navy retirement confirmation, verify scroll/pointer locking, then cancel or unmount. Releasing the global document immediately after the old cleanup and running queued callbacks failed both cases with the exact `ReferenceError: document is not defined` at bits-ui `resetBodyStyle` (`body-scroll-lock.svelte.js:34`, via lines 185/69); three existing tests passed. The document descriptor is restored in `finally`, even on failure. With asynchronous cleanup, the same command passes all five tests. The regressions also verify restored body styles before document release and zero remaining timers. This is controlled reproduction of the observed teardown path, not a claim about its natural failure rate.

**Acceptance: passed.** Both controlled cases finish cleanup before document release; all five rank tests and the full retained suite pass without unhandled errors. The task is complete; **02-02-01** is next and was not started. Phase 02 remains active, phase 03 inactive.

## Task 02-02-01a: Policy dispatch and Personnel write safety

September 16, 2026; working diff from `16047c179182f9213080114b5f0d6771522b1932`, preserving the pre-existing 02-01a rank-test/TODO/evidence changes. Node `v24.18.0`, npm `11.17.0` (declared `11.12.1`), existing installed Vitest `4.1.8`. Root AGENTS applies; hidden-file enumeration found no nested AGENTS. Read R1/R5, program decisions, verification/authz guidance and ADRs 0001/0002/0004. Split parent 02-02-01 into policy dispatch, authentication/admission, and private-resource/service-role children; only the first child is implemented here.

**Change:** Added 22 behavioral cases to existing `AD/handle.test.ts` and `ORG/api/personnel/server.test.ts`. The real dispatcher, policy evaluator and Personnel use cases execute against synthetic in-memory ports; authorization under test is not mocked. Expected rows are independently specified and cloned before seeding, so mutation cannot silently change the expected value. Denied dispatch must leave the record unchanged, never invoke the callback, emit a reason-coded denial and emit no success audit. Personnel denials preserve raw stored rows in both Organizations, create no deletion request and send no notification. No production behavior, schema, provider wiring or dependencies changed; this additive safety-net child replaces no implementation/configuration/docs and prunes no assertions. The queue's obsolete single-next-task guidance is replaced with bounded child dependencies.

| Retained contract                              | Named executable coverage                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Independent Critical Tasks/Rating Scheme lanes | `handle.test.ts`: `allows $lane writes with no $otherLane capability` and `rejects $lane writes despite configure capability in $otherLane`; allowed rows change once, denied rows never change.                                                                                                                                       |
| Multi-Group and ungrouped scope                | Dispatcher `rejects a multi-Group member writing $name`; Personnel `updates a record in the second assigned Group and preserves its Organization`, `rejects updates to $name without changing stored data`, and `rejects moving an accessible record to $name before mutation`.                                                        |
| Governance and owner-only actions              | Dispatcher `allows ordinary governance writes by an %s without feature capabilities`, `rejects governance writes by a configure-capable member`, and `rejects an admin attempting an owner-only operation`.                                                                                                                            |
| Identity-owned denial                          | Dispatcher `rejects identity-owned writes without an authenticated actor` and `rejects identity-owned writes to another user`. This is the policy seam's 403 contract; actual anonymous HTTP admission/session construction remains 02-02-01b.                                                                                         |
| Denied Personnel lifecycle writes              | Route `rejects $name for a viewer without changing records or creating approval requests`, covering actual POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH configs with configure access in every other lane. Existing allowed create/update/archive/approval/restore tests remain.                                                                              |
| Cross-Organization record access               | Route `rejects restoring another Organization record even for an Organization owner`: 404, foreign archived identity/values unchanged and no success side effects. This uses an Organization-filtering fake persistence port, not SQL/RLS proof; request membership admission remains 02-02-01b and SQL verification remains 02-03-04. |

**Read-only E2E inventory:** `permissions.spec.ts` contains member settings denial, owner/admin settings navigation and a Group visibility case with no assertion. The new governance cases plus existing route/layout policy tests protect policy decisions; navigation visibility and actual scoped read projections still need disposition in 02-02-01c before 02-05 deletes the harness. `z-auth.spec.ts` contains valid/invalid/empty login and logout checks; these remain explicitly owned by 02-02-01b. ## Task 02-02-01b-01: Verified session admission

September 16, 2026; working diff from clean `217fcb7776fb9e6881e1e9d99fa18f79fe8b3658`. Node `v24.18.0`, npm `11.17.0` (declared `11.12.1`), installed Vitest `4.1.8`. Read root AGENTS (no nested instructions found, including hidden paths), R1/R5, program decisions, testing/verification and auth/provider guidance. Split 02-02-01b into five enforcement seams; only verified session admission is implemented in this run.

**Change:** Added 19 cases in `src/hooks.server.test.ts`, exercising the actual hook and session/cookie helpers against synthetic Supabase responses and an in-memory cookie jar. Date is pinned to `2026-09-16T12:00:00Z` and restored after each test. The original five hook assertions and four helper tests remain. The fake HTTP event now has a mutable cookie jar and POST method; replaced its fixed cookie list and `never` cast. No production implementation, consumer, schema, dependency or configuration is replaced by this additive safety net; no assertion pruning or compatibility code. Superseded single-next-task queue guidance is replaced with bounded children.

| Retained R5 behavior / caller-visible failure                                  | Named executable protection in `hooks.server.test.ts`                                                                                                                                         | Why this seam adds protection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Anonymous access must not grant protected handler execution                    | `admits anonymous public requests without exposing a session`; `rejects anonymous protected writes without accepting another project cookie`                                                  | Existing helper tests only classify cookie names; the hook must actually admit public requests and redirect protected writes without calling the downstream handler.                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Sessions require valid session, verified claims and matching provider identity | `rejects a protected write when $stage returns no identity`; `rejects a protected write when %s reports a verification error`; `does not invoke the protected handler when %s is unavailable` | Three independent provider stages fail through the real admission pipeline. Expected login redirects/null identity or rejection and no downstream execution come from authentication requirements, not the provider stub's return value. Existing mismatched-user and untrusted-session-user tests remain.                                          |
| Stale/replayed credentials cannot admit a request or retain pending flow state | `clears stale credentials and denies replay after $stage reports $code`                                                                                                                       | Four explicit provider error codes cover returned and thrown failures, chunked-cookie removal, pending-flow cleanup and preservation of unrelated cookies. Prior stale-cookie coverage only exercised a public request after one thrown refresh error. Provider rejection is simulated; this does not prove Supabase token replay detection itself. |
| Absolute session lifetime revokes admission                                    | `admits a verified session before its absolute lifetime expires`; `revokes a session past its absolute lifetime before a protected write`                                                     | Independently specified sign-in timestamps just before/after 24 hours; expiry requires provider sign-out, cleared request identity/flow and login redirect before dispatch. No sleeps or copied timeout calculation.                                                                                                                                |
| A later request cannot inherit another request's identity                      | `does not reuse a verified identity or provider user storage in a later anonymous request`                                                                                                    | Both requests run through the same imported hook; the second cannot dispatch and has no session or provider-cached user. Helper shape tests cannot establish request isolation.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Provider cookie writes retain security attributes                              | `writes provider cookies through the hook with HttpOnly and root scope while retaining secure options`                                                                                        | Invokes the actual SSR cookie callback and checks the cookie write boundary: root path, HttpOnly, supplied Secure/SameSite/expiry preserved. Existing helper coverage would pass if the hook stopped using it.                                                                                                                                      |

These are the lowest-cost callable seams proving admission and cookie effects; no database or rendered UI is involved. Assertions cover public responses, identity and security side effects, and survive extraction/reordering of hook internals while the HTTP/provider contracts remain intact. No business use case is mocked as proof of authorization; absence of downstream invocation establishes that rejected requests cannot reach a route mutation. Organization membership/Group enforcement belongs to b-03 and SQL proof to 02-03-04.

**Detection:** Before edits, the focused command passed 3 files / 9 tests. The final focused command passes 3 files / 28 tests. Three temporary production faults each caused the selected new test to fail: ignoring a claims-provider error admitted a 200 response; extending the absolute timeout left an expired request on password recovery instead of redirecting to login; bypassing the cookie helper wrote `httpOnly: false` and `/auth` scope. All faults were restored with `finally`; `src/hooks.server.ts` has no diff. Fault runs used `-t` to select cases (other cases reported filtered); no skip declarations were added. Restored focused suite has no skipped tests.

```bash
npm run test -- src/hooks.server.test.ts src/lib/server/authSession.test.ts src/lib/server/authCookies.test.ts
```

Valid/invalid/empty login, native form validation and logout navigation are assigned to b-04; none is claimed transferred by this session child. MFA/pending-flow and user suspension admission remain b-02; membership/Organization suspension b-03; step-up/durable limits b-05. Read-only inspection found the hook logs an assurance-provider error and continues with `mfaPending = false`; b-02 explicitly owns investigation and resolution of this possible denied-request gap. No passing test is added to preserve that outcome and no complete MFA protection is claimed here.

## Task 02-02-01b-02a: Hook MFA and suspension admission

September 16, 2026; diff from clean `bba12bc9d7558b746c640034f51ed64e752c2fe8`. Node `v24.18.0`, npm `11.17.0` (declared `11.12.1`), Vitest `4.1.8`; existing dependencies, no install. Root AGENTS applies; hidden-file search found no nested instructions. Read R1/R5, program decisions, testing/verification, local-auth and auth/provider guidance. Used the diagnosing-bugs skill. Split b-02 into hook admission (this child) and MFA endpoint behavior (b-02b); endpoint mutations remain unimplemented in this run.

**Implementation:** The real hook now returns 503 before dispatch when authenticated MFA assurance lookup returns an error, throws, or lacks recognized current/next levels. It preserves the session so a later verified request can recover. Logout skips assurance lookup and still executes the existing sign-out/cookie cleanup action. Required suspension lookup now requires a boolean result; missing status denies with the existing 500 account-status response. Removed both permissive failure paths in place, without compatibility code or a new authorization abstraction. Updated current auth-boundary documentation. No endpoint, schema, dependency, configuration or unrelated guidance replacement; no assertions pruned. Pending-flow continuation routes and Organization/Group enforcement remain unchanged.

| R5 contract / recognizable failure                                         | Named protection in `src/hooks.server.test.ts`                                                                                                                                              | Why this seam                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Unverifiable assurance must not dispatch a protected write                 | `denies protected writes when MFA assurance is unverifiable: $label`; `denies protected writes when the MFA provider throws`                                                                | Real hook with fake provider; covers returned error (including accompanying data), missing data/levels and invalid level. Existing helper tests only classified levels and would miss hook fall-through. |
| Transient failure must permit retry without destroying a valid session     | `admits a later request after assurance verification recovers without discarding its session`                                                                                               | First request denies, cookie remains, later request dispatches after verification succeeds.                                                                                                              |
| MFA must block protected requests while permitting completion              | `redirects pending MFA before dispatching %s`; `allows pending MFA continuation at %s`; `admits completed %s assurance to a protected handler`                                              | Independent expected redirects and dispatch outcomes for Personnel, account, MFA factor management, verification/status/logout and completed AAL1/AAL2 sessions.                                         |
| Recovery/invite cookies restrict access and cannot bypass failed assurance | `restricts $flow sessions before dispatching $path`; `allows $flow completion at $path while MFA is pending`; `does not let a %s cookie bypass an assurance lookup failure`                 | Real cookie reading and gate integration, with forbidden cross-flow paths and permitted password/finalization routes. This proves hook admission, not endpoint mutation safety (b-02b/b-05).             |
| Logout must work during assurance failure                                  | `allows real logout during an assurance outage with flow %s`                                                                                                                                | Calls the actual logout action through hook dispatch with normal/recovery/invite cookies; requires provider sign-out, cookie removal and home redirect.                                                  |
| Suspended or unverifiable account cannot dispatch protected mutations      | `blocks a suspended user before the protected mutation at %s`; `denies a protected write when suspension status is missing`; `denies a protected write when the suspension provider throws` | Covers Organization and global API mutations, missing status and thrown failure; existing returned-RPC-error test remains. Completion/suspended-page/logout positives prevent blanket denial.            |

Requirements and expected responses come from retained R5, not copied hook calculations. Rejection tests assert no downstream handler invocation, the hook's enforcement boundary. Tests survive helper extraction or provider-call rearrangement that preserves HTTP/provider contracts. Existing b-01 identity, cookie, expiry and replay protection remains. No SQL or browser protection is inferred from mocks.

**Regression evidence:** `npm run test -- src/hooks.server.test.ts src/lib/server/mfaGate.test.ts` failed before the fix: 12 failed / 58 passed. Errors/missing assurance and missing suspension status admitted 200 responses, thrown assurance had no controlled response, and assurance outages prevented logout. After the fix the focused tests passed. An intermediate type check caught the generic hook event being passed to the route-specific logout action; corrected its route/params before final verification. No temporary production fault, debug probe, skipped test or waiver remains.

## Task 02-02-01b-02b-01: MFA endpoint request admission

**Final status: complete.** Initial blocked attempts below are historical; the final September 17 acceptance section records completion on the same runtime/test candidate.

September 16, 2026; diff from clean `494fb3dfc1f45077749a20ecf16905c8dfc5542c`. Node `v24.18.0`, npm `11.17.0` (declared `11.12.1`), Vitest `4.1.8`; existing dependencies, no install. Read root AGENTS, the active queue, R1/R5, program decisions, testing/verification and auth/provider guidance; no ancestor or nested AGENTS files found. Used diagnosing-bugs. Split b-02b into request admission (this child) and factor lifecycle/provider failures (b-02b-02), because request gating and the six handlers' factor transitions have separate enforcement seams. Only request admission implemented.

**Implementation:** All `/api/auth/mfa/**` requests now require verified session identity, including status and challenge verification. The hook checks suspension for enrollment, enrollment verification/cancellation and disable; suspended or unverifiable accounts cannot dispatch those handlers. Authenticated status/challenge verification still support pending MFA and recovery even during suspension. Invite restrictions and logout remain intact. Replaced the MFA portion of the blanket auth-API exemption in place and updated the auth-boundary document. No compatibility wrapper, handler lifecycle replacement, test pruning, schema, dependency, configuration or UI edits. Organization/Group authorization remains untouched.

**R5 safety map:** The new `MFA endpoint request admission` block in `src/hooks.server.test.ts` invokes the actual hook and all six actual handlers with synthetic provider dependencies; neither guards nor handlers are mocked. Denial assertions cover both dispatch and factor-changing provider calls. This is the least expensive seam proving enforcement before an endpoint; existing direct-handler tests and generic hook/helper cases did not establish it. Expectations describe public responses, redirects and prohibited effects and survive provider wiring or hook-helper refactors.

| Contract / recognizable failure                                     | Named executable protection                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Anonymous or stale identity must not reach MFA handlers             | `requires verified identity before dispatching $path`; `rejects stale provider identity before dispatching $path` across all six endpoints                                                         |
| Active accounts must retain factor management and completion        | `dispatches an admitted account to the actual $path handler` across all six; `allows an active AAL1 account to begin its first MFA enrollment`                                                     |
| Suspended/unverifiable accounts must not change factors             | `blocks suspended factor management at $path`; `denies $path without factor changes` for missing status, error with data, and thrown lookup across all four management endpoints                   |
| Pending MFA/recovery/invite must restrict factor changes            | `cannot change factors through $path` across all four management endpoints and the three incomplete-auth states                                                                                    |
| Completion paths remain reachable without opening factor management | `lets a suspended pending-MFA account complete via $path` for status/verification in login and recovery; `keeps invite sessions on their own completion path at $path`; retained real-logout tests |
| Unverifiable MFA assurance cannot dispatch any MFA handler          | `denies unavailable assurance before dispatching $path` across all six; prior hook tests retain missing/malformed/thrown assurance coverage                                                        |

### Reconnection retry and T3 timeout investigation

September 17, 2026; no further application edits. Recreated the same disposable project only after verifying its seed-disabled configuration and unused ports; fresh startup and `npm run build` both exited 0. Recreated synthetic fixtures (including MFA-account recovery) and served through `tailscale-preview vite preview`. The unchanged final runtime/test candidate remains the hash above, with all required checks already passed.

After Robert fully restarted T3 Code on the Mac and reconnected, browser controls returned in `tab_c` (same T3/Chrome/Electron versions). Signed in as the synthetic MFA account. At AAL1/AAL2, all four management POSTs redirected to `/auth/mfa-verify`; status remained readable. Invalid-code submission via Tab to Verify, then Enter, displayed the provider rejection. Temporarily suspended this fixture and completed a fresh valid MFA challenge with the same keyboard controls: status became AAL2/AAL2 and the app redirected to `/suspended`. After removing only that fixture's suspension, fresh account-security navigation displayed Enabled. This completes pending-MFA admission/completion evidence, including suspended completion; it does not grant Organization access.

Recording `1dfe8356-8d25-42fa-adde-8ab8459400ab-d1251f65-fc1f-40c7-ba05-7e39c1061e91-mp4.mp4` under the previously named attachments directory records keyboard invalid-code handling. No valid OTP was recorded. A subsequent sign-out click was followed by another host disconnect, so its final browser outcome and the attempted recording export remain unverified. Successful disable, final logout/anonymous recheck, and recovery completion remain open.

**T3 diagnosis:** The private app URL returned HTTP 200 while the Mac remained online, but repeated preview tools reported no host. Read-only installed-code inspection and sanitized server spans identify an optional post-action `status` call with `timeoutMs: 500`; its timeout disconnects the entire broker host before the toolkit catches the optional metadata failure. The 16:23:02 UTC trace shows that timeout/disconnect immediately before the persistent no-host failures. Reopening the Browser panel sent successful focus RPCs but did not restore automation. No T3 code/configuration was modified. At Robert's subsequent explicit request, submitted [pingdotgg/t3code#12273](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/issues/12273) using its bug-report template; the local report file was then removed at his request. The report distinguishes observed behavior from unconfirmed trigger details and omits credentials/private hostnames.

**T3 report accuracy:** The unavailable-host failures occurred during normal use, before any recovery intervention. The captured 500 ms timeout/disconnect and installed-code findings remain evidence for the reported defect. The exact cause of the slow metadata response and a deterministic reproduction remain unresolved. Removed the earlier mistaken attribution from the linked report; no desktop crash is claimed.

September 17, 2026; base `78c1baf82dee912a141163d3315d57b6593da649`. ## Task 02-02-01b-02b-02b: Enrollment creation and confirmation

September 17, 2026; base `cc72d7f796c316b6b144a0c4013c1cc0aee33ca3`. Only this ready leaf implemented; no nested AGENTS apply. **Cutover:** Creation keeps account selection with the authenticated provider and fixes the factor type to TOTP; rejects missing/malformed factor ID, type or QR results and catches provider exceptions. Confirmation validates JSON/string inputs and six digits, checks the authenticated provider's `all` factors for a pending TOTP match, honors lookup errors over accompanying data, and rejects verified/non-TOTP/unknown/foreign IDs before challenge. Account Security, the sole enrollment consumer, requires explicit `{ ok: true }`, preserves QR/code/factor on failure, blocks duplicate submissions and disables the code field while requests run. Updated hook fixtures and auth-boundary docs; replaced unsafe paths in place, no shims/pruning/config/schema changes. R1–R7 scope, data and auth admission remain unchanged.

Rendered `enrollment confirmation` cases prove retained enrollment on code/provider/network/malformed-success failures, serialized submission and clearing only after success; creation failure permits retry. These are the lowest seams proving handler effects and UI state. Expectations use independent pending/verified/foreign examples and survive handler extraction or incidental markup/copy changes. Existing actual-hook cases retain identity, incomplete-auth and suspension denial protection. No SQL enforcement claim.

## Task 02-02-01b-02b-02c-02: MFA challenge verification

September 17, 2026; base `bc9a6d4a0681f1896100215a9a9b7f9acc93120a`, initially clean worktree. Only this ready leaf implemented; no applicable nested AGENTS. Read root instructions, R5 and related retained contracts, program/auth boundaries, testing/completion and Svelte/UI guidance. Used diagnosing-bugs and tailscale-preview. Svelte MCP docs/autofixer unavailable; consulted [official state docs](https://svelte.dev/docs/svelte/$state), installed Supabase implementation/types and [challenge/verify docs](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/auth-mfa-challengeandverify).

**Cutover:** Verification validates JSON and a six-digit string; selects only the authenticated provider's verified TOTP factor, ignoring client-selected account/factor IDs; honors lookup errors over accompanying data; catches provider exceptions; and requires usable session tokens before reporting success. Public success contains only `{ ok: true }`. Login MFA and password recovery are the only runtime consumers: both serialize submissions, disable code input while pending, retain the code on failure and advance only on explicit success. Login now validates the response body and awaits safe continuation; recovery catches network failures for visible retry. Replaced unsafe paths in place and updated auth-boundary documentation. No shim, pruning, schema/configuration change, provider-boundary move or Organization/Group authorization change. Status reporting remains with c-03.

**Behavioral proof:** Existing four handler cases and three consumer redirect cases lacked malformed input, lookup data-plus-error, missing challenge-result and failed-submission coverage. New `verified-factor challenge boundary` cases exercise the actual handler with synthetic/stateful providers: authenticated factor selection, unchanged sibling factors, no pending-enrollment confirmation, denial before assurance effects, provider invalid/expired/replayed-code rejection, missing session results, thrown failures and retry. Each consumer's rendered failure/retry cases prove code retention, no premature navigation/password form, duplicate-submission prevention, disabled input and advancement only after explicit success. Existing actual-hook tests retain anonymous, pending-flow and suspension admission protection. Injected provider replay/expiry errors prove handler rejection, not Supabase's internal OTP algorithm; no SQL enforcement claim. These seams establish distinct effects with independently specified factor/state/response outcomes and survive handler extraction or incidental layout/copy changes.

## Task 02-02-01b-02b-02c-03: MFA status reporting

September 17, 2026; base `38fce78d1a6393b020d84537eec4006dc9b1af15`, initially clean worktree. Only this ready leaf implemented. **Cutover:** Status returns only recognized current/next assurance levels. Provider errors take precedence over accompanying data (retained 401); absent/invalid results and exceptions return 503 without exposing private exception details. Recovery is the only runtime status consumer: it independently validates the response, keeps password entry blocked during unavailable status and exposes a serialized retry. An established recovery session is retained; exchanged hash credentials are removed before checking status and are not exchanged again on retry. Session expiry still follows the login redirect; absent recovery credentials still show an unavailable link. Valid status selects password entry or the retained MFA challenge. Replaced unsafe null defaults, permissive consumer parsing and the failed-status dead end in place; updated auth-boundary guidance. No shim, pruning, schema/configuration change, provider-boundary move or Organization/Group authorization change.

New actual-handler cases establish password-only, MFA-required, MFA-completed and already-elevated current/next outcomes; missing/null/unknown results; data-plus-error precedence; exception containment and retry. Rendered recovery tests establish blocked password entry, visible retry, pending-control/duplicate-request protection, redirect handling and established/hash-session reuse. Expectations come from R5 assurance and recovery contracts; tests survive handler extraction or incidental copy/layout changes. Existing actual-hook tests retain anonymous/pending-flow/suspension admission protection. No SQL enforcement claim.

## Task 02-02-01b-03-01: Request membership context and cache

September 17, 2026; base `dd76e3a2055f32e1ae79bf242d5c4e3b3ccf8121`, initially clean worktree. Read root AGENTS (no nested AGENTS), queue, R1/R5, program decisions, authorization/provider boundaries and testing/completion guidance. **Scope and behavioral proof:** Added `adapters/membershipAdmission.test.ts` and `membershipContextCache.test.ts`. The former exercises real `getApiContext`, membership queries/projection, HTTP/layout/prefetched context construction, scoped store, scope enforcement and route policy dispatch. The installed Supabase SDK uses a local fake HTTP transport over synthetic rows; the fake applies emitted filters and projections, with no simulated RLS. Only the admin-client factory and existing billing-disabled flag are substituted; authentication, membership lookup, context construction and policy functions are not mocked. No network or database connection occurs.

Named cases cover anonymous denial even with a seeded grant; missing, wrong-user and other-Organization membership; owner/admin Organization scope; canonical slug identity; independent lane overrides; both allowed Groups and exclusion of other-Group, ungrouped and foreign Personnel; allowed writes and unchanged Personnel on capability/scope/admission denial; malformed storage; unknown/unavailable Organization lookup; failed membership lookup and retry. Cache cases protect concurrent route/layout/slug/UUID reuse, prefetched-layout reuse, user/Organization keys, revocation on the next request, shared in-flight failure and stale-failure eviction races.

These assertions implement R1/R5 caller-visible identity, access and mutation outcomes. Nearby context tests mock `getApiContext` and return unconditional query rows, while route tests start from preconstructed contexts; they cannot establish the complete admission/filter chain. The new transport seam supplies independently specified identities and records, survives internal context/helper extraction, and checks observable records rather than source text or collaborator layout. Query counts are limited to the request-snapshot/deduplication contract. No pruning or runtime replacement was necessary; no obsolete code/config/docs were introduced or left from this test-only cutover.

**Fault detection:** Initial focused run failed two allowed-write cases because the synthetic operator lacked the required Personnel `configure` override; corrected the fixture to the existing action contract. Controlled faults then each exited 1: removing the membership Organization filter admitted an unauthorized write; removing user identity from the cache key reused an owner grant; removing Group filtering exposed excluded/ungrouped rows in all three context paths; unconditional failed-promise eviction lost a replacement membership. Each fault was applied singly and restored in `finally`; runtime diff is empty. Commands were `npm run test -- <new-test-file> -t <case-title>`; exact titles/outcomes are in `/tmp/t3-stabilization-membership/fault-{org-filter,user-cache-key,scope-wiring,stale-eviction}.log`. ## Task 02-02-01b-04-03: Logout revocation

**Cutover:** The shared POST action previously ignored revocation errors, cleared pending-auth state and redirected as success. It now requires explicit `{ error: null }`, contains provider exceptions/malformed results as generic 503 failures, and clears pending state only after success. The hook also defers pending-cookie cleanup to this action when POST logout cannot verify identity; it still clears untrusted request-local identity/flow state. All five existing native consumers—TopHeader account menu, dashboard chooser, MFA verification, recovery and invite acceptance—use this endpoint unchanged and receive the same retry page. Supabase retains ownership of session-cookie removal and global refresh-session revocation, including already-missing-session handling. Issued bearer tokens retain provider expiry semantics. Absolute session expiry in the hook and password-change `scope: 'others'` revocation are separate retained contracts.

Deleted the old unconditional success path in place and replaced its redirect-only POST rendering; updated provider-boundary guidance. No obsolete parallel implementation/configuration remains. No pruning, shims, waivers, skipped tests, dependency/schema changes or billing scaffolding. E2E files remain read-only pending their owning removal task.

Rendered retry-page coverage checks an announced failure and enabled native POST submit. Hook tests prove normal/recovery/invite failure and retry remain reachable for a suspended account during assurance outage. ## Task 02-02-01b-04-04: Token session establishment

September 17, 2026; HEAD `bf612cfb53fe9209d5e194c4c4e7152de9cba884` plus the preceding completed logout task already in the working tree (starting runtime/test hash `396c8cfa85a7681980c3c7a4770b9d180ef57d9a5d0c74f09aed9b4e54f01f68`). Preserved that work. Read root AGENTS, nested discovery (none), queue, retained R1–R5 contracts, prior evidence and applicable operating, architecture/ADR 0004, testing, Svelte/UI and completion guidance. Used diagnosing-bugs and tailscale-preview. This task closes b-04; b-05 remains unstarted and Phase 03 inactive.

**Completed cutover:** The endpoint rejects malformed JSON, non-object bodies, missing/non-scalar/whitespace tokens and unknown flows before authentication. Explicit success requires matching usable provider user/session data; errors take precedence, unavailable/exception/malformed results return generic 503, rejected credentials return generic 401, and responses are `no-store`. Supabase can remove stored credentials after a rejected refresh, so this endpoint now uses a request-local SSR client with staged cookie writes. Only validated success publishes token-only, rooted HttpOnly session cookies and the pending flow. Existing cookies survive denial; an omitted flow cannot clear a pending restriction. The hook also defers pending-cookie cleanup to this POST when identity lookup is unavailable while keeping request-local authentication untrusted.

Both fragment consumers now share token parsing/exchange behavior, prefer incoming credentials over a different existing account, keep password completion blocked on failure, and serialize visible retries. Success clears the fragment and continues through existing recovery MFA status or invite password completion. Replaced the unchecked cast, raw provider-error response, unconditional success, direct cookie effects and both unhandled one-shot fetch paths in place. Removed the now-stale `locals.supabase` baseline entry; the endpoint remains an allowed auth-provider edge, with no claim of provider-neutral migration. No schema/dependency changes, compatibility shims, waivers, skipped declarations, relaxed checks or billing scaffolding.

**Behavioral proof:** Real-handler tests exercise malformed requests, missing/data-plus-error/mismatched provider results, exceptions, cookie preservation and retry. Actual SSR/GoTrue integration with a synthetic HTTP boundary proves rejected refresh replay cannot remove an existing session, successful refresh publishes token-only cookies, and valid repeats retain the flow. Rendered tests cover both consumers' network/provider/non-JSON/null failures, disabled retry serialization, unchanged credentials on retry, explicit-success continuation and incoming-link rejection despite an existing account. Hook regression proves unavailable identity plus denied exchange preserves the pending cookie without granting authentication. These R5 effects are tested at callable boundaries with independently specified outcomes; no function under test is mocked, and incidental layout/copy refactors remain possible.

## Task 02-02-01b-05-01: Durable limiter provider boundary

September 17, 2026; base `c74c3e834dd94529cdb08973e1b868a37b8335df`, initially clean working tree. **Cutover:** The real Supabase adapter previously interpreted `{}` as an allowed request and accepted ambiguous rows or unusable retry metadata. It now requires explicit successful provider status, exactly one row (or the existing single-object form), a boolean decision and nonnegative safe-integer retry milliseconds. Provider errors take precedence over data; zero retry still preserves denial. Removed the unchecked RPC result cast/interface and truthiness fallback in place. Every existing policy already uses this adapter and propagates its failure before protected effects; no consumer migration, compatibility implementation or policy-budget change is needed. No tests pruned, schema/dependency changes, bypasses or governance exceptions.

**R5 behavioral map:**

- `durableRateLimit.test.ts`: missing/malformed/ambiguous results, data-plus-error and thrown failures, valid allow/deny/zero-retry, retry after provider recovery, and failure of a later dimension. The public adapter/store seam establishes decision validity; it does not assert SQL durability.
- `durableRateLimitConsumers.test.ts`: the real adapter through all seven policy modules (ten entry points: access submission, setup renewal/completion, recovery, password update, signup, invitation create/resend, platform approval/resend). Independently specified retained window/attempt budgets reach the RPC; raw identifiers stay hashed, normalized identities share a bucket, separate actors/policies remain separate, and provider failures never become permission. Existing per-policy tests retain limit/reset and Organization/actor/recipient isolation cases.
- `passwordUpdateRateLimit.test.ts`: rotating IPs cannot escape the user budget, rotating users cannot escape the IP budget, unrelated identities remain usable, and checks resume after expiry. These are application orchestration tests using a synthetic store; actual SQL reset/concurrency enforcement remains 02-03-04.
- `api/auth/update-password/rateLimit.test.ts`: real POST → real policy → real adapter, faking only provider RPC/auth and audit effects. Malformed decisions stop normal/recovery/invite password checks, updates, revocation and pending-cookie deletion; a valid retry succeeds. Later-bucket errors also stop all effects. Independently limited user/IP results return 429 and rounded `Retry-After`, including zero at the reset boundary. Existing handler tests mocked the limiter and could not detect this adapter failure.

These tests protect independently required R5 outcomes at the lowest useful callable seams and survive helper/layout/copy changes. RPC input assertions protect security budgets and identity partitioning, not incidental call trivia. No function under test is mocked.

## Task 02-02-01b-05-04b-01: Password proof and session isolation

September 17, 2026 (Mac browser timestamps September 18 UTC); base `b4d9498e25021adb7a7c81127fb3bfb9c5d60d82`, initially clean. Read root/ancestor/nested instructions (no additional files), R5, prerequisite admission/confirmation evidence, canonical auth-provider contract/ADR 0004, testing and completion rules. Used diagnosing-bugs and tailscale-preview. Split b-05-04b into proof/session isolation and mutation-time MFA/continuation; only the first child is completed. Phase 02 remains active.

**Cutover:** Deleted unchecked old-password sign-in through the request client. Proof now uses a separate nonpersistent provider client, validates returned actor/session/tokens/finite future expiry, verifies identity and recognized assurance for the exact issued token, and revokes only its temporary session. Malformed results, data-plus-error, exceptions, failed cleanup and expiry cannot reach mutation or replace browser cookies. Mutation uses the original hook-bound client and rechecks its expiry after proof, preserving an existing AAL2 session instead of replacing it with password-issued AAL1. Existing limit budgets, password bytes, recovery/invite markers and suspension policy remain unchanged. No wrapper, configuration exception or additional provider dependency remains. Canonical boundary documentation is updated.

**Detection and tests:** `npm run test -- src/routes/api/auth/update-password/proof.test.ts` initially reported **40 failed / one passed** against the original route (`/tmp/t3-password-proof/red.log`). Final coverage also binds the issued token to independently fetched identity, handles failed temporary-session revocation and retry, and detects expiry during assurance/cleanup. The real-SDK cookie regression was run against the original route and failed (`sdk-red.log`); the candidate was restored immediately and passes. Its HTTP fake exercises actual Supabase sign-in/storage/logout/update behavior: rejected proof preserves the cookie jar; accepted proof locally revokes its own token, mutates with the original AAL2 bearer, revokes other sessions with that original bearer, and never stores the proof token. Real-hook dispatch and adjacent admission/limiter fixtures now supply complete provider facts without removing assertions. The expanded route/hook run passed **334 tests**, then the standalone SDK regression passed. These observable R5 outcomes add protection absent from prior error-only proof stubs and survive helper extraction or UI copy/layout changes; no SQL enforcement is inferred from fakes.

**Consumers:** Audited Account Security, recovery and invite pages. The first displays rejected HTTP `{ error }` responses and retains fields; recovery/invite do not submit old-password proof and retain their existing flow. ## Task 02-02-01b-05-04c-02b-01: Confirmed password revocation continuation

September 17, 2026 (browser September 18 UTC); clean base `c09cb027e8db6eb94d43bbfe3895f5c98abe35f4`. **Cutover:** Explicit revocation acknowledgment is required. Failed, malformed or thrown results return truthful `202 session_revocation_pending`, retaining the recovery marker. Signed HttpOnly receipts bind confirmed mutation to actor/provider session/flow/original expiry. Received receipts restore only revocation continuation or completed replay across all three loaders/consumers; password drafts are discarded after acknowledgment. Fresh MFA, unchanged abuse limits, serialized retry, focus, expiration and explicit Account Security restart remain enforced. Invite activation rejects pending/expired/unverifiable completion before profile or membership effects; accepted logout clears the receipt (the global SDK denial distinction is recorded below). The existing signer is required in every environment with a separate signing context. Removed log-and-success revocation failure, the transient success branch and received-partial handling that could invite another password submission; no compatibility wrapper, scaffold, schema/dependency change or relaxed guard.

## Task 02-02-01b-05-04c-02b-02: Server-persisted password attempt reconciliation

September 18, 2026; initially clean base `13c46fc00f465037f5198b393183081ade5b70f3`. Read root/applicable instructions (no nested files), R5, prerequisite completion evidence and relevant authentication/testing/UI/verification contracts. **Complete, including production-browser acceptance through the Tailscale preview follow-up.** Phase 02 remains active; no subsequent child or phase was activated.

**Cutover:** The service-only `password_change_attempt` RPC admits one attempt per actor before provider mutation, bound to session/flow/original expiry. The ledger stores no passwords, hashes or tokens; browser roles and direct service table access are denied. A consumed `ttt_password_attempt` metadata marker plus transaction-local password witness confirm the ledger inside the provider transaction. Row locking makes reconciliation observe commit or fence the attempt, so late writes roll back. Failed provider responses never imply rollback. Confirmed changes resume revocation only; its acknowledgment persists before recovery-cookie cleanup. All three loaders and rendered consumers restore durable state, discard drafts on unknown outcomes, serialize status/restart actions, retain MFA/focus and handle expiry. Invite activation now requires a completed record, including when no receipt was delivered. Removed signed receipt generation/parsing/cookie authority, signer dependency, receipt-specific fixtures and uncertain-outcome password resubmission. Canonical auth/architecture/component docs reflect the final behavior; no compatibility wrapper, new dependency, bypass, allowance or reduced scan.

**Behavior evidence:** Final focused auth/hook/loader/component run: **553 tests / 20 files**. Existing proof, MFA, rate limits, suspension and session-cookie checks remain. Tests cover durable denial/malformed results, concurrent admission, committed-but-lost responses, completion-write failure, session/flow binding, expiry, replay, activation denial and every rendered reconciliation consumer. The activation-without-record regression failed against the old guard and passed after cutover (`finalizer-red.log`, `finalizer-green.log`). SQL controlled fault: disabling only the disposable confirmation trigger as its administrative owner failed **8 of 30** assertions; restoration passed all 30 (`db-red2.log`, `db-restored.log`). The first disable attempt lacked table ownership and changed nothing; its passing run is not fault-detection evidence.

**Real provider/concurrency:** GoTrue `v2.188.1`, installed Supabase SDK, isolated synthetic account/session. `/tmp/t3-password-attempt/provider-contract.mjs` passed 12 concurrent admissions with exactly one owner, metadata-only denial, a committed password response deliberately dropped by transport, restored pending state, marker consumption, explicit revocation/completion, rejected-call fencing and rejection of a delayed marked write while retaining the confirmed password. `concurrency.py` held an actual provider transaction open, observed reconciliation waiting on its row lock, then verified it read `pending` after commit. No browser suite or application bypass was created/run. This provider-specific transaction assumption is also visible in [GoTrue's transaction implementation](https://github.com/supabase/auth/blob/v2.188.1/internal/api/user.go); it is not an SDK-response idempotency claim.

**Database:** Disposable `/tmp/t3-password-attempt/isolated`, project `t3-password-attempt-02b-02`, verified unused ports 60120–60124/60127/60129 and inspector 8283 (corrected from the initially recorded 9501). Inspected lifecycle/seed configuration; copied migrations/templates/tests and the actual package file, with seed disabled and empty paths. Initial default image `17.6.1.106` reproduced the known permission-denial SIGSEGV even for the existing session-provenance function. Recreated only owned resources with `.temp/postgres-version=17.6.1.155`, following the previously recorded [upstream fix](https://github.com/supabase/supautils/issues/214); no denied assertion or grant changed. CLI `2.101.0`, PostgreSQL `17.6`. `supabase start --workdir /tmp/t3-password-attempt/isolated` replayed the old chain through `20260630001400`; inserted a synthetic retained identity/profile, applied `20260630001500` using isolated-container `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1`, and verified unchanged ID/email/metadata. `npm --prefix /tmp/t3-password-attempt/isolated run db:test` passed **14 suites / 236 assertions** after upgrade. `supabase stop --workdir /tmp/t3-password-attempt/isolated --no-backup`, then start with the complete chain through `20260630001500`, and the same package script passed **14 / 236** after fresh replay. Existing tenant/RLS contracts remained included. No existing local or remote database was accessed or reset.

## Task 02-02-01b-05-04c-03: Invite finalization provider completion

September 18, 2026; clean base `6d90fb2a4eee7bdff5945b3a76dfe9a7f75bd09f`. Root/ancestor/nested instructions inspected (no additional files); read the active task, relevant R3/R5 and prerequisite evidence, canonical auth/provider, testing, Svelte/UI and verification contracts. Used diagnosing-bugs and tailscale-preview. **Complete, including production-browser acceptance below.** Only this task was worked; its completed children close c and b-05-04. Outer parents remain unchecked and Phase 03 inactive.

**Changed/deleted:** Profile writes require an acknowledged row matching the actor and submitted values; profile reads distinguish confirmed absence, supported nullable legacy fields and malformed/unbound results. Account Profile uses the same validated save/load path. Invitation lookup validates every page before any acceptance and orders the pending snapshot; every acceptance result is checked. Provider failures preserve the pending marker and confirmed partial memberships; retry reads the remaining invitations. Reviewed setup still gates acceptance, expired/blocked/missing invitations grant no membership, and confirmed existing membership/replay does not count another grant. The route rechecks expiry before effects and marker removal. The invite consumer requires explicit activation acknowledgment, retains activation-only retry, exposes reviewed setup recovery and unavailable-invitation outcomes, and reconciles an already-cleared marker after response loss. Reload uses hook-verified identity/pending state and offers dashboard continuation when no activation is pending. Removed discarded provider errors, unchecked profile acknowledgment, unverified loader `getSession`, stale activation-only reload behavior and its obsolete governance exception. No compatibility wrapper, no-op, schema change or new dependency remains.

## Task 02-02-01b-05-05 Account email-change proof and completion

**Scope/cutover (September 18):** Account Security now binds email changes to the hook-verified actor and unexpired session, validates scalar form fields without truncating email or changing password bytes, and enforces durable user/IP limits of five attempts per fifteen minutes. Password proof moved from the password route into `server/passwordProof.ts`; both mutation consumers use its existing identity/token/expiry/assurance validation and explicit-token local revocation. Removed request-client password sign-in, unchecked provider acknowledgment, raw provider errors, duplicate route-local proof and the form callback that discarded error/retry state. Fresh assurance for the original token gates mutation; pending MFA preserves the draft through a separately identified inline challenge and requires explicit retry. Success requires the matching provider user, unchanged current email and exact pending address. Confirmation instructions cover both inboxes; errors/lost responses never claim rollback or completed change. No obsolete wrapper, configuration scaffold, dependency or schema change remains.

**Discovered and completed within this task:** Browser confirmation exposed `buildTrustedUserFromClaims` restoring an older JWT email over the freshly verified provider email. The real-hook regression failed with the old address in both downstream user and session state. Once the hook binds provider identity to the verified subject, every request consumer now receives that provider email while retaining the original actor/token/MFA. This fixes both reload presentation and subsequent current-password proof; no stale-email fallback remains for the bound user. Recorded in this task’s TODO scope rather than deferred.

**Behavioral evidence:** All 25 initial action regressions failed against the original implementation (`/tmp/t3-email-change/red.log`); the later hook freshness regression independently failed (`email-freshness-red.log`). Actual Supabase SSR/JS transport proves denied proof cannot replace cookies, temporary revocation never revokes the admitted session, and allowed email mutation retains AAL2 tokens. Existing password-route regressions exercise the extracted implementation. Rendered tests cover focused field retention, unavailable/malformed responses, serialized submission, explicit MFA retry, password clearing on acknowledged success and pending-state reload. These are independently specified R5 outcomes at real callable/interaction seams; no function under test is mocked, no assertion pruning or skipped tests. Final focused command: `npm run test -- src/routes/account/security src/routes/api/auth/update-password src/lib/auth/password src/hooks.server.test.ts src/lib/server/authSession.test.ts` — **520 passed / 18 files**.

## Task 02-02-01c: Private resource and service-role safety

**Scope (September 18, 2026):** Added `AD/privateResources.test.ts`, exercising actual exported document list/upload/download/archive/restore/delete and sign-in roster download/upload/unlink/delete/create HTTP handlers through real context construction, policies, scoped stores and Supabase SDK/adapters. No runtime defect was found or runtime cutover needed. This is additive safety coverage, with no assertion pruning, dependency/config changes or obsolete implementation to delete. Corrected the authorization document’s stale claim that sensitive reads require only `operate`: the retained application catalog and existing tests require `configure`; artifact writes still require `operate`. No authorization semantics changed.

**Behavior and boundary evidence:** 61 new cases bind private resources to the admitted user, Organization, parent Personnel record and complete Group scope. Anonymous/nonmember/wrong-lane requests reject before private writes/signing or privileged snapshot lookup; denial auditing remains allowed. Foreign/mismatched document IDs, ungrouped Personnel, mixed-Group and Organization-wide rosters cannot sign, upload, remove or mutate private objects/metadata. Allowed downloads in the second assigned Group use the caller client and a 300-second signature. The training-only roster creation path uses its narrow service-role Personnel lookup, then caller-client persistence; foreign or out-of-scope lookup results cannot become a response or stored snapshot. Document listing exposes only the requested person’s metadata and omits storage paths; metadata/signing provider failures return no successful URL. Expected fixtures are independently specified, and pre-request records are cloned before comparison. Transport filtering has no implicit RLS: removing emitted Organization/record filters is observable. This is application/adapter proof, not SQL/RLS proof.

The lowest seam for these missing guarantees is the real HTTP handler with fake transport: existing document use-case tests cover file validation, storage caps and upload rollback, while existing roster tests cover replacement/unlink failure recovery; neither proved actual request admission and caller/service wiring together. These new checks survive route-internal helper extraction and copy/layout changes because they assert HTTP outcomes, retained records and provider effects. Controlled faults were applied one at a time and restored in `finally`: substituting the service client for route storage caused **two failures**; removing the Personnel binding from document lookup caused **one failure** (another person’s document became signable). ## Task 02-02-02: Account setup safety net

**Scope (September 18, 2026):** R2/R5 safety tests only, based on ADR 0009 and the reviewed-acquisition contract. No production behavior, schema or UI changed.
