Existing production implementations remain the exercised subjects, with fake persistence/auth/delivery ports and controlled time; no network or database effects.

**Executable contract map:**

| Contract                                                                                                    | Named executable protection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Anonymous reviewed entry; authenticated MFA/invite/recovery continuation                                    | `auth/sign-up/page.server.test.ts`: GET/POST reviewed redirects and `preserves $location on GET and POST before reviewed-access redirect`; new `auth/request-access/page.server.test.ts` entry cases; renewal loader continuation cases.                                                                                                                                      |
| Approval cannot issue twice after review race/replay; provider failures do not deliver unissued credentials | `UC/platformAccessRequestAdministration.test.ts`: `does not issue a credential when approval loses a review race or is replayed`, lookup/approval/issuance failure cases; existing sensitive-action denial, throttle, rejection and completed-request denial cases.                                                                                                           |
| Approval/delivery recovery, public origin, no raw credential in audit                                       | Same application suite: `keeps approval recoverable after a thrown delivery failure and retries through resend`, existing delivered/failure/resend/origin cases; `accessRequestSetupDelivery.test.ts` and `authEmailOutbox.test.ts` cover durable delivery outcomes.                                                                                                          |
| Normalized identity, 90-day renewal, unrelated request preservation                                         | `accessRequestSetupDelivery.test.ts`: `renews only the normalized email identity while preserving another approved request`, persistence failure, exact renewal boundary and quiet missing/pending/rejected/completed/stale cases; renewal route invalid-input, throttle and indistinguishable public acknowledgments.                                                        |
| Hashed 14-day credentials, approval required, replacement/revocation/claim states                           | Existing `accountSetupLinks.test.ts` state/claim cases; `accountSetupCompletion.test.ts`: `rejects the raw setup credential at the exact fourteen-day expiry without account effects`.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Signed ten-minute session; submitted identity cannot choose another account                                 | `accountSetupSessions.test.ts`: signature tampering and exact expiry; setup route tokenless exchange/load, mismatched request/link denial, posted raw-token denial and `cannot replace the signed setup identity with submitted email, request, link or raw token fields`.                                                                                                    |
| One account mutation during competing attempts; stale sessions/replay denied                                | `accountSetupCompletion.test.ts`: `allows only one in-flight setup session to mutate credentials and rejects replay after completion`, `invalidates an exchanged setup session when a fresh link replaces its credential`; existing wrong-request and failed-claim/profile/completion/sign-in cases. Fake atomic claim proves application orchestration, not SQL concurrency. |
| Setup/confirmation creates no membership                                                                    | `accountSetupCompletionSupabase.test.ts`: completion permits only profile/request persistence and explicitly denies `organization_members`; confirmation route tests cover signup/invitation redirects without acceptance and provider-rejected replay without restoring pending flow.                                                                                        |

**Prune dispositions:** `DELETE` the setup route's `does not render a hidden raw-token form field` source scan and its filesystem/path imports. It only matched markup spellings; existing tokenless-load/exchange tests protect the actual absence of raw credentials in page data, and new action tests reject raw-token substitution. `REWRITE` the adapter's membership-negative assertion (previously the nonexistent `organization_memberships` table) and session lifetime expectation derived from the production constant. ADR 0009/R2 require no membership effects; R5 requires the signed, ten-minute session boundary. The callable adapter and session reader now fail for unauthorized persistence or a changed lifetime using independently named tables and literal UTC times. These are the lowest seams proving those contracts, add protection the old assertions lacked, and survive internal refactors or copy/layout changes. No suspect assertion was retained unchanged.

### Synthetic SQL fixture specification for 02-03-04 and 06-01

Include an unlinked generated fact as a separate row.                                                                                             |
| April 1 outside-window slot, Charlie person/Group slots, second-Organization slot                                                | Preserve every row; deny cross-Organization and out-of-Group reads/writes while allowing Alpha+Bravo person and Group cases. One Organization/type/date slot remains unique.                                                    |
| Computed-only event/slot on March 3 with no `daily_assignments` row                                                              | Never invent a persisted assignment from an overlay. Its removal cannot affect any saved row.                                                                                                                                   |
| Referenced manual/generated types, availability/status, special day, `clinic_role`, rank reference and independent signed roster | Retain identities and values, roster snapshot/personnel scope/file metadata and applicable rank-reference protection. Include an unreferenced generated type for separate disposition; do not infer deletability from its name. |

02-03-04 must exercise the actual RLS/grants, denied writes with unchanged rows, unique-slot/type constraints and application-independent reads. 06-01 carries the same immutable-field baseline into fresh/upgrade preservation proof for 06-02/03. ## Phase 02 queue reassessment

Robert approved revising the queue after the read-only reassessment. Base `9fef8f6fb354fa2be09150161555b687ac056e12` plus this documentation diff. Root instructions apply; no nested `AGENTS.md` found. Used writing-for-agents; the preceding prune-tests audit supplied recommendations, not executed deletions.

**Decision:** Replace thirteen remaining Phase 02 tasks with four: bounded retained-coverage gap closure (02-03-02), unchanged substantive database proof (02-03-04), named assertion cleanup (02-04-01), then complete E2E removal (02-05). The six 02-03-03 children and three 02-04-02–04 rows are superseded, not marked complete. General residual test review transfers to **13-03**, now an explicit prerequisite of 14-01; it must close before program completion. No later phase is activated.

**Why:** Remaining feature-wide review descriptions overlapped existing application, component and domain tests and serialized that review before any harness removal. Several E2E specs are small smoke flows; the Group-visibility case in `permissions.spec.ts` has no assertions. Expanding each into comprehensive feature testing is not required to transfer its unique retained contract. Earlier authentication work did fix consequential defects, including SDK-suppressed global sign-out denials; completed evidence and tests remain valid.

**Coverage credited for the next task:** These are inspected executable assertions, not a claim that every feature or browser journey is certified. Existing evidence can close a mapped requirement; extra cases require a named uncovered failure.

| Retained contract                                              | Existing evidence to reuse                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Calendar slot identity, edits, Group scope and stale responses | Completed [02-03-01](#task-02-03-01-persisted-calendar-preservation); `UC/daily-assignment-slots/index.test.ts` also restores an existing slot after a replacement insert fails. SQL atomicity/reference semantics remain 02-03-04 and F02 repair remains 11-02.                                                                                         |

All RLS consumers cut over through the existing signatures. No compatibility helper or alternate path was added. Remote application of this migration is a separate operator action and was not performed.

## 2026-09-18 — In-app browser regression pass after PR review

**Candidate and outcome:** `1cf9d992c07ee737db1b3ee3b4c7fe386777a967`, PR #1289. Production-mode in-app browser verification found an unresolved **P1 Calendar Group-assignment compatibility blocker**. This supersedes the preceding entry's no-blocker assessment. No runtime fix was made during this verification pass.

**Confirmed blocker:** `DailyAssignmentModal.svelte:84` and `MonthlyAssignmentPlanner.svelte:59` create Group options with the name as both label and value. Their consumers pass `groupsStore.names`. Migration `20260918000100_enforce_calendar_assignment_group_scope.sql` requires `daily_assignments.assignee_id` to equal a same-Organization Group UUID. In the actual browser, an owner opened September 19's Daily Assignments, selected Bravo for a Group-targeted assignment, and received **Internal server error**; the previous assignment remained. The existing UUID-based fixture displayed a UUID rather than its Group name. A separate synthetic legacy row with assignee `Alpha` remained in the database but was absent from the owner's Calendar range response. A scoped member's direct browser request with the Alpha UUID succeeded; the Bravo UUID returned 403. Thus the positive scope restriction works, but the UI/persistence contract is incompatible. The monthly planner has the same mismatch by source inspection; its full flow was not separately executed.

**Migration consequence:** Do not treat the Group-scope migration as safe to apply before a compatible application and legacy-data strategy are ready. Current main has the same name-valued pickers. Applying the migration can reject existing UI writes and hide previously saved name-based Group assignments. It does not delete those rows. Corrective work needs to preserve saved assignment identities, resolve legacy Group references safely, use stable IDs throughout the affected consumers, and verify old/new deployment ordering. No remote migration, deployment, merge, or production-data inspection occurred.

**Environment:** In-app T3 browser tab `tab_w`, Electron 44.1.0 / Chrome 152.0.7977.65, desktop 1280 × 800. Private origin `https://omarchy.tail930090.ts.net:8444`, local TLS 4279. Disposable directory `/tmp/t3-browser-regression-1cf9d992/isolated`, project `t3-browser-regression-1cf9d992`, unused ports 61520/61521/61522/61523/61524/61527/61529 and inspector 9523; Postgres 17.6.1.155. Copied the full migration chain through `20260918000200`; disabled seed loading; enabled local TOTP enrollment/verification and email confirmations. Started with `supabase start --workdir /tmp/t3-browser-regression-1cf9d992/isolated`; fresh migration replay/start succeeded. No existing 543xx/553xx/563xx data was accessed.

**Build and transport:** `python /tmp/t3-browser-regression-1cf9d992/run.py npm run build` exited 0, followed by `python /tmp/t3-browser-regression-1cf9d992/run.py tailscale-preview vite preview`. The wrapper supplied `PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=http://regression.localhost:61521`, disposable provider credentials, the private HTTPS app origin, both billing flags false, empty Stripe credentials, synthetic Account Setup/Resend values, and `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL=noreply@example.test`. A scratch Node fetch wrapper captured Resend delivery offline, blocked nonlocal providers, and allowed a controlled HTTP 503 for the Group creation failure check. It did not bypass application authentication, authorization, CSRF, or rate limits. No repository browser harness was added. Local optional Vercel telemetry MIME errors and Electron preload diagnostics did not prevent application interactions.

**Fixtures:** Three synthetic confirmed accounts (owner, member, outsider). The owner created Browser Regression Unit through the UI, with the normal Leadership/Alpha/Bravo defaults. The owner added SPC Synthetic Browser to Alpha through Personnel. The isolated fixture then added a Bravo person, an Alpha-only operator membership with Calendar operate capability, and Group-targeted assignments for Alpha and Bravo. The outsider account was created but its cross-Organization flow was not executed. Provider recovery and real TOTP codes came only from this disposable stack; no tokens, secrets, or token-bearing URLs are recorded here.

## 2026-09-18 — Calendar Group assignment regression fixed

**Scope and candidate:** Fixes the immediately preceding browser finding on base `6f1be720`. Daily and monthly Group controls now submit stable IDs and display current Group names; Calendar month/three-month headers, dashboard, retained Today Breakdown component and the Group store lookup contract consume IDs. No name fallback, scope relaxation, replacement harness or remote operation was added. A forward migration converts unambiguous same-Organization legacy names in place and refuses missing, foreign or ambiguous references before changing rows. Deployment remains a coordinated cutover: old main is not compatible with the new representation. The [migration runbook](../migrations/calendar-group-assignees.md) documents the preflight, ordering and rollback limits.

**Regression protection:** The new daily-picker interaction failed before the fix with `group-alpha` displayed instead of Alpha Renamed, then passed. The monthly integration submits both individual and quick-fill Group IDs through the real store transport; header/dashboard tests assert renamed labels. These cover the observed interaction and caller-visible payload at the lowest seams, not internal markup or implementation scans. Existing Calendar person coverage remains. Focused checks passed 39 tests in four files. The isolated SQL contract also proves a rename preserves the saved Group ID and visibility and rejects name-valued writes, including for owners. An initial expected-ID error in this new SQL assertion was corrected to follow its fixture's earlier Bravo-to-Alpha edit; the behavior assertion was retained.

**Database verification:** Two distinct disposable stacks: `/tmp/t3-calendar-group-fix-6f1be720/isolated`, project `t3-calendar-group-fix-6f1be720`, ports 61620/61621/61622/61623/61624/61627/61629 and inspector 9623; upgrade directory `/tmp/t3-calendar-group-fix-6f1be720/upgrade`, project `t3-calendar-group-upgrade-6f1be720`, corresponding 617xx ports and inspector 9723. Ports checked unused, seeds disabled, Postgres 17.6.1.155, Supabase CLI 2.101.0. Fresh full migration replay/start succeeded. The upgrade stack started with the audited `origin/main` chain (`05a247ef`), persisted four legacy/person/UUID fixtures across two Organizations, then applied all five branch migrations with `supabase migration up --local --workdir /tmp/t3-calendar-group-fix-6f1be720/upgrade`. All four identities and every non-assignee field remained byte-for-byte equivalent as JSON; exact same-name Groups resolved to their own tenant IDs and person assignments remained unchanged. Removed only those disposable fixtures before running independent suites. `npm run db:test` from each disposable directory passed **18 suites / 347 assertions**.

**Migration edge checks:** `supabase/migration-tests/calendar_group_assignees.sql` executes the actual migration against synthetic legacy fixtures, not a copied conversion. All **9 TAP assertions** passed: tenant-specific conversion, exact field preservation, idempotence and full atomic rejection of missing Group, foreign Group ID and UUID/name ambiguity. Executed via disposable-container `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atf` after copying both migration/test directories. Verified successful exit, plan `1..9`, all nine `ok` lines and no `not ok`. An initial attempt to run this include-based test with `supabase test db` failed because the CLI did not copy its relative migration dependency; the separate documented container invocation resolves that limitation.

**Production browser evidence:** Production build and `tailscale-preview vite preview` on `https://omarchy.tail930090.ts.net:8444`, local TLS 4279; T3 tab `tab_x`, Electron 44.1.0 / Chrome 152.0.7977.65, 1280 × 800. Distinct provider origin `http://calendarfix.localhost:61621` isolated auth cookies. Build/runtime used disposable provider credentials, private HTTPS app origin, both billing flags false, empty Stripe credentials and synthetic Account Setup/Resend values with the external-only local transport wrapper from the preceding pass. No secrets or token URLs are evidence artifacts.

- Owner opened the converted slot: picker and date header showed Alpha. Changed it to Bravo through the picker; database held the Bravo UUID and reload retained Bravo without an error.
- Renamed Bravo through Personnel Group management using Enter. Calendar header and dashboard immediately resolved the existing ID to Bravo Renamed after navigation/reload.
- Monthly planner saved Alpha for September 19, then quick-filled weekdays. Reload retained 23 ID-valued Alpha assignments (22 weekdays plus the selected Saturday), with readable labels.
- Alpha-only operator saw no Bravo personnel/assignment, selected Alpha through the daily picker for September 27, and retained it after reload. Direct out-of-scope Bravo assignment returned 403. Attempting to replace an invisible existing Bravo slot with Alpha was rejected with a generic 500 rather than 403; the saved Bravo row remained unchanged. That existing error presentation remains a limitation, not a successful 403 claim or a data mutation. The in-scope and out-of-scope rows were verified directly in the disposable database.
- Saved synthetic screenshots: migrated picker `browser-screenshot-omarchy-tail930090-ts-net-mu7kvjt3-e5d947f1.png`, daily save `browser-screenshot-omarchy-tail930090-ts-net-mu7kwo7v-75ffebe3.png`, monthly persistence `browser-screenshot-omarchy-tail930090-ts-net-mu7kynbb-9f885c32.png`, scoped persistence `browser-screenshot-omarchy-tail930090-ts-net-mu7l0q17-0b15630d.png`, under `/home/waffleflops/.t3/userdata/browser-artifacts/`; scoped screenshot visually inspected. **Local isolation incident:** One rerun was mistakenly launched from the repository directory and reached the existing local `trooptotask-v1-maintenance` database. It failed in the first transactional `account_setup_claim.sql` test when a PostgreSQL backend segfaulted (23:14:06 UTC). PostgreSQL terminated other sessions, recovered automatically and reported ready within a second; Docker health was healthy, with no container restart/OOM. The test had not committed, so PostgreSQL recovery rolled back its fixture writes. No reset or migration ran there, and no remote/production database was targeted. The mistake was disclosed immediately. Subsequent commands explicitly used disposable working directories; only container status/logs were read to assess the incident, with no further queries or repairs against the existing database. This was an execution error, not an isolated passing test or a claim that the existing local stack was untouched.
